REFINERY NEWS: India's Vizag refinery runs normally after fire incident
Refinery: Vizag (Visakhapatnam), Andhra Pradesh, India
Owner: Hindustan Petroleum Corp. Ltd.
Overall capacity: 300,000 b/d
India's state-run HPCL-owned Vizag refinery is operating normally after a fire incident, as the refinery was preparing for the landfall of Cyclone Murasu, company officials said Nov. 3.
The fire was assessed to have broken out at the sulfur pitch tank of the residual upgradation facility at the refinery on Nov. 2, the officials said.
However, the fire was immediately brought under control without any human casualties in a challenging scenario of Cyclone Murasu's landfall on the east coast at a speed of 110-120 kilometers/hour, the officials added.
The cyclonic storm over the Bay of Bengal made landfall on the east coast on the morning of Nov. 3, they said.
The Murasu is the second cyclone after the very severe Cyclone Arnab, with a speed of 150 km/h, that hit the east coast on Oct. 28, weather officials said.
Vizag processed 268,000 barrels/day in September, down 15% year over year and 303,000 b/d in the April-September period, up 1.3% over the year-ago period, according to the oil ministry update.
For fiscal year 2025-2026 (April-March), Vizag processed 320,000 b/d, up 4.6% year over year, it said.
-- Ratnajyoti Dutta
REFINERY NEWS: Russia's Yanos damaged after drone strike
Refinery: Yanos, Yaroslavl, Russia
Owner: Slavneft
Overall capacity: 15 million mt/year (300,000 b/d)
Infrastructure damage occurred at Russia's Yanos refinery after a large-scale drone strike involving 124 drones, the Yaroslavl regional governor Mikhail Evraev said Nov. 3 in a Telegram post.
Emergency services are working on extinguishing the fire, he also said.
The Yanos (Yaroslavl) refinery has been operating normally recently, according to Russia-based market sources with knowledge of the matter. It has been operating partially after a Sept. 17 attack.
-- Elza Turner

Platts launches LR1 Persian Gulf-Singapore 60kt clean tanker freight assessment Nov 2, 2026
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has launched a new Long Range I {LR1} Persian Gulf to Singapore 60kt clean tanker freight assessment, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
This decision follows extensive industry consultations and analysis of spot fixtures and market data available since 2019, as well as direct feedback from market participants.
An analysis of spot fixtures on the key routes from the Persian Gulf to Singapore and from West Coast India to Singapore

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

22

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

November 3, 2026

indicates that increasingly more concluded spot fixtures were based on 60kt cargo sizes. Over 2019-2025, on the Persian Gulfto-Singapore route, 77 observed spot fixtures were concluded on 60kt cargoes in the LR1 class of clean tankers. On the West Coast India-to-Singapore route, 223 spot fixtures were concluded for 60kt cargoes over the same period.
LR1 vessels typically load between 55,000 mt and 65,000 mt of clean product, including naphtha, gasoline, jet fuel, and gasoil. Platts has observed LR1 vessels frequently being fixed on a spot basis on the Persian Gulf-Singapore route using 60kt stems, particularly for shipments of jet fuel and gasoil.
The new assessment reflects a 60kt clean product cargo loading from the Persian Gulf and discharging in Singapore, using modern LR1 tonnage with an age below 15 years. Platts will assess the route based on observed fixtures, as well as vessels going on subjects, bids/offers, and freight indications obtained through market survey of shipowners, charterers, and shipbrokers.
The assessment is published on a Worldscale percentage basis, together with a corresponding price in $/mt. The $/mt figure is derived from a flat rate based on a basket of key load and discharge ports.
The route basket consists of: Jubail-Singapore Ruwais-Singapore Ras Tanura-Singapore The basket flat rate used to calculate the $/mt freight will be based on the average of the Worldscale flat rates for the three routes. Platts will continue to assess its existing suite of benchmark Clean Persian Gulf-Japan 55kt LR1 assessments. The new assessments appear in Clean Tankerwire, Monthly Clean Tankerwire, Platts Shipping Alert pages 330 and 331, and Platts Tanker Alert pages 2922, and 2923. Assessments follow a 16:30 Singapore timestamp and the Singapore holiday calendar. Please send any feedback or questions to shipping@spglobal.com and pricegroup@spglobal.com. For written comments, please provide a clear indication if they are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make those not marked as confidential available upon request.

Pricing information for material with other specifications, such as PEN 60/70, may be considered for the assessments, normalized to the standard.
The assessments reflect market value at the close of Asian trading at 4:30 pm Singapore time and follow the Singapore publication schedule. Bids, offers and trades reported throughout the day may be considered in the assessment process, with values normalized to the market close at 4:30 pm Singapore time.
This follows a proposal published Sept. 14 and available here and a decision note published Sept. 28 and available here.
The new assessments will be available on Platts Global Alert and Platts Refined Products pages 2004, 2522, 2502 and 2503. They will be published in Asia Pacific Arab Gulf Marketscan and Oilgram Price Report.

Platts amends freight calculation for Oman, Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has amended the freight calculation used in its daily Oman and Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
Under the change, Platts is using the daily VLCC Gulf of Omanto-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (DOOEW00), launched Aug. 3, to calculate the outright prices of Oman and Murban delivered into North Asia at 4:30 pm Singapore time (0830 GMT).
The change better reflects shipping economics for the two grades, with Murban primarily loading from Fujairah and Oman from Mina Al Fahal outside the Strait of Hormuz.
Previously, the freight calculation was based on the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (TDAAB00).
Platts first proposed the change in a subscriber note published Sept. 28, 2026, followed by a decision on Oct. 15, 2026.
The freight calculation continues to use the metric ton-tobarrel conversion factors applied to the grades, in line with their prevailing qualities. The conversion factors are currently 7.32 for Oman and 7.62 for Murban.
In line with this change, Platts is also using the daily VLCC Gulf of Oman-to-Far East Worldscale freight assessment (DIOEW00) in

Under the current MPP methodology for Minas, Cinta and Widuri, Murban -- as one of five basket grades -- is normalized for delivery to Singapore, accounting for freight. This was previously calculated using the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Singapore worldscale freight assessment (PFAOD00).

Platts to amend rollover date of Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will amend the rollover date of its Asian Dated Brent (ADB) Strip at London close assessment to the first publication day of each month, effective Dec. 1, 2026.
The change follows a review of historical data sets and will align the ADB Strip at London close assessment with the current rollover date of the ADB Strip at Asia close assessment.
Currently, this assessment, which reflects the forward assessment of Dated Brent two months ahead of the date of publication at the 4:30 pm London close, rolls over on the 9th of the month.
Platts proposed this change in a subscriber note published Sept. 21, 2026.
The assessments affected by this change appear in Crude Oil Marketwire, Asia Pacific/Arab Gulf Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, Platts Global Alert/ Platts Crude Oil Alert pages 2206, 2213, 2215, 2217, 2219, 2260, 2261, 2262, 2263, 2264, 2265, 2266, 2267, 2268, 2269, 2270, 2271, 2280, 2281, 2558, 2559, and under the following price database codes:

Platts to launch forward derivative assessments for STS Lome gasoline, diesel and jet fuel
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has noted increasing demand for transparency on West African refined products, both on a spot market basis and for forward delivery. In response, Platts will start publishing daily forward swap assessments for the existing Gasoline STS Lome, Low Sulfur Diesel STS Lome, and Jet Fuel STS Lome physical price assessments, effective Nov. 30, 2026. These will be published as Month 1, Month 2, and Month 3 assessments.
In order to assess the derivatives on an editorial basis, Platts will consult a range of market information, including information heard in the physical market, relevant forward exchange-traded data, as well as related markets.
The published assessments will correspond with delivery dates three months forward, starting with the front month [M1], followed by M2 and M3.

Platts to launch STS Lome Gasoline differentials to Eurobob FOB AR assessments
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch a new daily price assessment reflecting the differential between the Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment, effective Nov. 23, 2026.
This follows increased demand for transparency on West African gasoline differentials, as Nigeria's Dangote refinery continues to reshape flows and pricing dynamics in the Atlantic Basin.
The assessed differential is calculated as the difference between the physical Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the midpoint of the three-to-10-day forward strip, itself derived from the established Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment derivative assessments.
The new assessments will provide a transparent indication of the value of STS Lome gasoline relative to Northwest European gasoline blendstock assessments, and will complement Platts existing outright STS Lome gasoline differentials against Platts Premium 10ppm FOB Mediterranean cargoes and Premium 10ppm FOB Rotterdam barges.

Platts will publish the new assessments in $/mt, timestamped at 4.30 pm London time, as well as a monthly average.
The new gasoline assessments will be found in European Marketscan and on Platts Global Alert and Platts Refined Products Alert fixed page 1122.

Platts amends Abu Dhabi crude OSPs publication Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has updated its publication
of Abu Dhabi crude official selling prices effective Oct. 8, 2026, in line with changes announced by Abu Dhabi National Oil Co. (ADNOC).
In July, ADNOC said it would adopt a prompt-month pricing methodology based on the Platts Dubai benchmark (PCAAT00) plus an ADNOC-announced differential for the OSPs of its Abu Dhabi onshore and offshore crude grades, with effect from November 2026 loadings. ADNOC added that it would announce the OSP differentials for Murban, Das, Umm Lulu and Upper Zakum in the month prior to the delivery month.
Accordingly, Platts will publish the monthly OSP differentials for the four grades, basis Platts Dubai, beginning with the November differentials issued by ADNOC on Oct. 8, 2026.
Platts first announced the change in a subscriber note published on Aug. 31, 2026.
The OSP differentials are published under the following reinstated codes:

Grade Murban Umm Lulu Das Blend Upper Zakum

OSP Diff to Dubai AAIOS00 OSLUD00 AAXPL00 AAIOV00

Platts previously published the OSP differentials under these codes until May 2021, when they were then suspended due to a change in the ADNOC pricing methodology.
From November, the existing codes for the outright OSPs for Murban (AAIKZ00), Upper Zakum (AAILC00), Das Blend (AAXPH00) and Umm Lulu (OSLUF00) will remain unchanged. Platts will publish the outright OSPs of the four grades following the publication of the average of Platts Dubai crude assessments during the month of loading.
In view of the changes, Platts is no longer updating the codes reflecting Upper Zakum, Das Blend and Umm Lulu OSP differentials against Murban, as well as the codes for all four grades, including Murban, calculated against the monthly average of Platts cash Dubai 1st Line assessment in the month of trade.
The following codes will no longer be updated from October:

Grade Murban Umm Lulu Das Blend Upper Zakum

Diff to Dubai AOSPA00 AOSPB00 AOSPC00 AOSPD00

Diff to Murban OSP
AOSPE00 AOSPF00 AOSPG00

ADNOC's OSPs are published in the Platts price database and on Platts Global Alert page 1051.

3-DAY TRAINING

Oil Markets Fundamentals & Commercial Strategies

Nov 3-5, 2026 | Singapore

Dec 8-10, 2026 | Dubai

Mar 23-25, 2027 | Singapore Apr 6-8, 2027 | Houston

Apr 27-29, 2026 | London

May 11-12, 2027 | Abuja

Jun 22-24, 2027 | Istanbul

Jun 30-Jul 1 | Kuala Lumpur

Oil markets are increasingly complex, shaped by shifting supply and demand, geopolitical developments, changing trade flows and price volatility. This course provides a practical understanding of the forces shaping global crude oil and refined product markets.
Explore market fundamentals, price formation and forecasting, trading, and risk management, and learn how to translate market developments into better commercial decisions.
Build the market knowledge and commercial insight to make more confident decisions in a rapidly evolving oil market.
WHO SHOULD ATTEND The course would be suitable for:
· Technical and non-technical professionals working across crude oil and refined products
· Traders, brokers and market analysts · Professionals in commercial, planning, business development,
supply and distribution roles · Shipping and logistics professionals · New industry entrants seeking a practical understanding of oil
markets

Instructors
Kaushik Burman Roy Global Director, Energy Training, S&P Global
Ha Nguyen Head of Oil & Fuels, Long-Term Research, S&P Global
Daniel Colover Head of Oil, Chemicals and EMEA Markets Engagement Program, S&P Global

2-DAY TRAINING
Upstream Fundamentals and Economics
June 2-3 | Houston

Fossil fuels dominate the energy mix representing over 80% of global energy demand. With geopolitical tensions influencing supply volumes and lower price, the S&P Global Commodity Insights "Upstream Fundamentals and Economics" course will equip you with the insights and strategies needed to navigate a constantly changing environment, meet challenges with informed decisions, and drive business growth.
Our prestigious Upstream Fundamentals and Economics Training is a great opportunity to hear from a range of S&P Global experts sharing their expertise, regional trends and industry insights, as well as concrete case studies and analyses.
WHO SHOULD ATTEND
This course would be suitable for:
· Technical and non-technical professionals, with experience or new recruits, from crude oil and refined products companies
· Governments, EPCs, OEMs, Suppliers, Investors · Personnel from Projects, Planning, Business Development,
Commercial
